So (a) O112's timing shares (SNAP 0.008 of band years before year 12, HYB 0.816) and PAUSE-S128's LOC and FIRST describe the post-allowance part only [T:overclaim if quoted as the hold]; (b) 'S370 rarely pauses' (O83), the one 'for' of LOSS-TABLES in the 6 Oct 01:52 receipt, is false under a count that includes the allowance-only draw (grade B); (c) on S370 the saved paths show the same signature as S130 (HYB rushes at access 0.095 against PCLSI 0.062, then holds at 0.187 to 0.225 spending its other pots 189k to 62k while PCLSI keeps about 110k; P-LO holds at 0.714 to 0.739 in years 17-18, other pots 123k to 38k). (a) scorePaths reads T.survived[i] (reduce-7t.mjs l.232), and makeTrace has no survived field. pensionDeathTaxRate is 0 on every library household (PR5), so ref7t is always computed and every job, the screen and the reach included, throws at its first world. (f) reach-hodep.sh runs the 30-point reach jobs at scale 2: round(30 / 2) = 15 points, not the 30 its header, the item file and my receipt name. (g) The plant 'score' is caught by the ref7t comparison, not by its 'failed path never above zero' clause: early failures accrue enough floor-year cuts to stay at or under zero. Require every (world, year, q) cell, print each slice's count, and refuse a short slice. (2) want() takes a plant as caught if it fails anywhere (l.69), not inside its slice. Plant top is caught by the wrong fault: with the fix off, readV returns 0 at A = 0 and below the foot (adaptive.js l.86, l.88), so year 4's A = 0 and A = 50 cells fail from O177's defect whether or not the top is NaN, and O178's guard is never shown. Require plant top to give a non-finite p at a year-4 cell above the foot (A of 1,000 or more), where only the NaN top acts. Require plant foot to fail at the years 1-3 A = 0 cells. (3) The top fixture is not shown to reach O178's case: print year 4's bill and the later bills and assert 3 rem + d0 under the floor, the bridge years unchanged and year 4 still the inflow year; otherwise 'top passes, finite' may have run on nothing. (4) No plant acts inside the fix's real slice in the base fixture. That slice is year 4's live A = 0 node (V0 > 0), year 3's step height and year 4's lattice; plant foot changes only nodes that are already 0 (years 1-3 at A = 0, where the fix is inert by construction). Add the reference's own plant 'drift' (adaptiveLattice.plant, no code edit). It grows worlds 0 and 2 at the centre's rates in the backward step (adaptive.js l.97, l.120, l.150) while simulate grows at cs[k] (l.212), so it moves V0(4), the year-3 step and the year-4 lattice in worlds 0 and 2. It must fail at a year-3 or year-4 cell in world 0 or 2. (5) The gate's form: per slice, print the count, the base's largest clean gap, the tolerance, and each plant's effect inside its slice, with effect over tolerance and tolerance over clean gap each at least 2. The test prints per-cell gaps only. Q1, THE 0.05 ALLOWANCE [T:design]: defensible as a materiality bound where p is smooth (judged, grade C: O177's p error near 1 gave 2.1 points, so 0.05 scales to about 0.1 against the 0.5 margin). Not defensible at year 3's cliff. There p jumps between two adjacent lattice nodes, from 0 (no move pays year 3's bill, so Q = 0) to about V0(4) (the post-flow state reads year 4's A = 0 node through footRead) - grade A from the code. The lattice cell is about 2% wide in A; the test's log grid steps 8% (121 points over 4 decades). So a cliff cell either lands in the smeared cell (honest gap up to about V0(4)/2, far past 0.05; roughly even odds over three worlds) or collapses onto the step's top. Simulate's nearest-cell move just above the step can also be the default move 0 of a node where nothing pays, a mismatch that is simulate's own artifact. Fix: locate each year's step by bisection; bind at A* e^(+0.08) and A* e^(-0.08) (outside the smear), with 4 se plus a measured allowance; report the in-cell gap without binding. Keep q-crossings found by bisection in years 1-2, where p is smooth. Q2, b AND GAIN [T:design]: at A = 0 the state is b-free (setState makes both pots 0), so one b is right there. At the cliff and year 4's A = 50 the low-money states S362 visits sit near b 0 or 1 (one pot drawn first; K1F switches between ISA-first and taxable-first), not 0.5, the least visited low-money state (the CORNERS and SLICES lines). Use b 0, 0.5 and 1 and all three gain nodes there; simulate is cheap beside the build. O179-SECOND is outside this test at any b or gain: it lives in the reader's copy and split, not in p; a reader-level probe (K1F's GB by corner status) reported in HO-DEP is its only window. Add year-4 cells across the lattice (A of 500, 5,000, and either side of the old top 3 rem + d0): the top's part of the fix, NOT CHECKED since O177's resolution. Q3, p AGAINST simulate() [T:design]: right for F1 as my receipt defined O179-FIXOWN - stored values against the true survival of the reference's own rule. simulate reads none of V or V0 (only the moves MV and MV0, the flow and the growth), so a value fault shows. It cannot see faults shared through setState's fixed context (pension 0, typical sheltered cash), F.flow or F.grow (O165's p against the engine), the candidate's runs, or a wrong move choice (both sides take it). Name these limits in the gate's line. 4 POINTS: sound (grade B). The lattice reads bills and the three gain nodes; FOOT-S366's logs show the same 160x41x3 lattice and 24 moves at 4, 30 and 60 points. OTHER: a plant that 'fails to build' counts as caught; the top fixture builds first on the same inflow, so only a fix-off-specific exception can count - require its message to name the top. ENFORCEMENT [T:enforce]: check-plan's gate-test check (l.432-441) accepts any receipt naming the file and blob, whatever its verdict, so this NOT-MET receipt would satisfy it if cited at this blob. Proposed for the maintainer: require a verdict word (e.g. GATE-READY) in the receipt. RANKED, unchanged (no new outcome): O179-TRADE, O179-SECOND, O179-FIXOWN, O179-SEED, O179-OTHER; DEP-ADVCAL, DEP-NONE, DEP-WORSE, DEP-OTHER. CAUSES OVERLAP: two questions (O179's carrier; what the fixed reference adds over the order reference on deposit cliff households), each set summing to 1. REQUIRED 1, PLANT TOP CANNOT BE SHOWN [T:plant][T:code]: with the inflow at 200,000, 3 rem + d0 is negative (year 4's bill about -193,000 against three times later bills of about 7,000 each, judged from S362's bills; the test asserts the case without saying which form it takes), so year 4's lattice is NaN throughout (lo + (NaN - lo) i / 159). read() calls simulate at every cell, including the Y4 cells whose p is NaN. There nearest() returns MV[4][NaN] (Math.max(0, NaN) is NaN) and moves[undefined], and F.flow reads c.acts[undefined].lump (fast.js l.386): a TypeError. It escapes run() into the try around build() and run(); its message ('Cannot read properties of undefined') fails /top/NaN/lattice/i, so topCaught is false, the plant reads FAIL, and it is labelled 'the build refused'. Fix: in read(), take a non-finite p as off and skip simulate; keep the regex branch for build() errors only, with word boundaries (/top/ also matches 'stop'). REQUIRED 2, SMOOTH IS BOUND WITH NO PLANT AND AN HONEST ERROR NEAR ITS BUDGET [T:design]: year 3's p steps from 0 to about V0(4) across one lattice cell (no move pays below the bill; above it the post-flow state reads year 4's A = 0 node), grade A from the code. Year 2's p averages that smeared step over a year's shock, so at a year-2 crossing it carries an honest error of about f x Delta x V x /w - 1/2/ (f the shock density at the step, about 0.39/sigma; Delta the year-3 cell, about 0.0205 in log A from S362's bills; V the step height; w where the true threshold falls in the cell). With sigma 0.1 and V 0.8 (judged) that is up to 0.032 at the worst w and about 0.016 typical. Add the maximum noise over about 18 cells (about 2.5 se, 0.009), and the base's SMOOTH gap can exceed half its tolerance (0.064 / 2), failing 'tolerance at least twice the clean gap' honestly (judged risk 20-35%). And no plant is required in SMOOTH, so the slice can only fail, never show a catch. Fix: make SMOOTH reported, not bound and out of the ratio (CLIFF's year-3 cells above the step and Y4 see F1 directly), or bind it only with drift's effect there printed and required. MINOR: (a) the ratio compares the slice's largest tolerance with its largest gap (l.125); compare cell by cell (gap at most half that cell's tolerance): tolerances run 0.0502 at p near 0 or 1 to 0.064 at 0.5. (b) STEP 0.08 and INCELL's e^0.03 assume the lattice cell's width. Compute each year's Delta from billsOf (lo = log max(100, 0.5 d0), hi = log(3 rem + max(d0, 0)), Delta = (hi - lo) / 159); assert STEP at least 2 Delta; put INCELL at A* e^(Delta/2) (at Delta about 0.02, e^0.03 lies past the step cell, so the reported line would not be in it). THE COORDINATOR'S QUESTION, IS 'TOLERANCE AT LEAST TWICE THE CLEAN GAP' REACHABLE: A0 yes (p and sim both exactly 0 in years 1-3 at no money); CLIFF yes (cells below the step 0 against 0; year 3's cells above it are smooth - the averaged year-4 values, with no step in year 4 since the inflow pays it - quadrature and interpolation about 1e-3 plus about 0.01 of maximum noise; a small judged risk that the nearest node's best move has its threshold within half a cell above the cell); Y4 judged yes (no pay step at year 4, and year 6 to TL read exactly by clearStep); SMOOTH at risk (above). PLANT EFFECTS: foot about 1 against 0.0502 (p 1 against sim 0 at A = 0), a ratio of about 20. Drift reachable but not assured (judged about 75%): world 0's survival from year 4 is 1170 of 1505 against world 1's 1996 of 2175 (K1F, results/diagfoot case3), about 0.14 at typical states and more at low money, against a required 0.128; the test enforces it, so a shortfall fails honestly rather than passing vacuously.
